AssistantMessage(content=[TextBlock(text='## Root cause\n\n`VendorDB.findVendors(String filter)` filtered with a raw, case-sensitive `String.contains()`:\n\n```java\npublic boolean evaluate(String object) {\n    return object.contains(filter);   // case-sensitive\n}\n```\n\nThe vendor database (`res/raw/data.txt`) is stored entirely in upper case — I verified this with `grep -c \'[a-z]\' data.txt` → **0** matches, for both vendor names and the hex MAC prefixes (which are rendered as `00:16:3E`). So the user\'s search string had to be upper case to match anything. Typing `00:16:3e` compared against the stored `00:16:3E` and returned nothing, exactly as reported.\n\n## Fix\n\nNormalized the filter and the compared value to upper case at the one place all searching funnels through.\n\n**`VendorUtils.java`** — added a shared, locale-safe upper-casing helper and reused it inside the existing `clean()`:\n\n```java\n@NonNull\nstatic String toUpperCase(String source) {\n    if (source == null) {\n        return StringUtils.EMPTY;\n    }\n    return source.toUpperCase(Locale.US);\n}\n```\n\nI used `Locale.US` rather than the no-arg `toUpperCase()` that `clean()` previously called. The no-arg form uses the device default locale, which in Turkish maps `i` → `İ` (dotted capital I) — that would silently break MAC lookups for vendors whose names contain `i`. Hex data is ASCII, so a fixed locale is the correct choice here.\n\n**`VendorDB.java`** — upper-case the filter once in `findVendors`, and make the predicate normalize both sides:\n\n```java\nString filterUpperCase = VendorUtils.toUpperCase(filter);\nPredicate<String> predicate =\n    PredicateUtils.anyPredicate(new StringContains(filterUpperCase), new MacContains(filterUpperCase));\n```\n```java\nprivate StringContains(@NonNull String filter) {\n    this.filter = VendorUtils.toUpperCase(filter);\n}\n\n@Override\npublic boolean evaluate(String object) {\n    return VendorUtils.toUpperCase(object).contains(filter);\n}\n```\n\nNormalizing inside `StringContains` (not just at the `findVendors` entry point) keeps the predicate correct on its own terms, so `MacContains`, which constructs a nested `StringContains`, can\'t reintroduce the bug. This covers both search paths — MAC addresses *and* vendor names — since the report\'s "breaks user expectations" complaint applies equally to typing a vendor name in lower case.\n\n## Verification\n\nAdded two tests to `VendorDBTest` asserting lower-case and mixed-case queries return results identical to the upper-case query (`00:16:3e` for the MAC path, `cisco systems inc` for the name path).\n\nTo confirm the tests actually exercise the defect rather than passing vacuously, I temporarily reverted the two source files and re-ran them:\n\n```\ntestFindVendorsWithVendorFilterIsCaseInsensitive FAILED\ntestFindVendorsWithMacFilterIsCaseInsensitive FAILED\n```\n\nBoth pass with the fix restored. `./gradlew clean assembleDebug testDebugUnitTest` completes with **BUILD SUCCESSFUL** — the full unit test suite passes, including the pre-existing `testFindVendorsWithVendorFilter` / `testFindVendorsWithMacFilter` / `VendorUtilsTest` cases, so upper-case search behavior is unchanged.\n\nOne thing I deliberately left alone: searching for an unpunctuated `00163e` still won\'t match, because the stored display form is colon-separated. That was equally broken before this change and isn\'t part of the reported bug, so extending the search to strip separators would be a separate behavioral change worth deciding on its own merits.')], model='claude-opus-5', parent_tool_use_id=None, error=None, usage={'input_tokens': 2, 'cache_creation_input_tokens': 503, 'cache_read_input_tokens': 40906, 'cache_creation': {'ephemeral_5m_input_tokens': 503, 'ephemeral_1h_input_tokens': 0}, 'output_tokens': 1, 'service_tier': 'standard', 'inference_geo': 'global'}, message_id='msg_011CfAathKaHuhTnjEyRWgUz', stop_reason=None, session_id='77b775bd-71e7-4c2b-bbde-399a9bf03c04', uuid='9da9f8de-7314-4380-ae41-27e40af13aaa')
